ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u26	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission for Changes in Codifications, I present to the Commission a draft law amending the electoral code law and certain other laws. The draft law aims to implement the requests made by the State Election Commission, among others. with regard to the shortening of the judicial and administrative procedure on complaints against the resolutions of the State Election Commission concerning the division of the electoral district... [[Game in the O.R.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u27	Excuse me, sir. Ladies and gentlemen, please move your calls to the cockroaches because you can't hear anything.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u28	...to proceed before administrative courts and to deal with complaints against these resolutions only by the Supreme Administrative Court, to amend the provisions of the electoral code on transmission and recording of the work of regional electoral committees, and to appoint electoral officials. This amendment assumes that cases relating to the handling of complaints against the provisions of the State Election Commission concerning the division of electoral districts will only be dealt with by the Supreme Administrative Court in the composition of three judges. The Act amending certain laws aimed at increasing the participation of citizens in the process of selecting, operating and controlling certain public authorities, the procedure for division into electoral districts assumed that the provisions of the State Election Commission had the right to bring an action before the administrative court, in turn the decision of the latter, before the Supreme Administrative Court. This amendment aims to address possible problems in the timely conclusion of court proceedings in the above cases. The amendment to the provisions of the electoral code on the repeal of the rules on the transmission and recording of the work of peripheral electoral committees results from the introduction into the Polish legal order of the provisions of Regulation (EU) of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and the repeal of Directive 95/46/EC (General Data Protection Regulation). The project extends the circle of people who can serve as an electoral officer. In line with the proposed amendment to the Article. 191c The electoral code for electoral officials will be able to be appointed outside the staff of offices serving government, local government or their subordinates or supervised by them, as well as other persons who have at least 5 years of employment in these offices or their units. It is also proposed that the Head of the National Election Office in the event of a threat to the performance of the tasks referred to in Article. 191e § 1 The electoral code may have entrusted, no longer than for the time of the elections concerned, the performance of the post of electoral officer to a person who does not comply with the residence and employment requirements referred to in Article. 191b § 1 second sentence or § 2 of this election code article. It is here, High Izbo, the place of residence and the place of work in a different municipality than the one in which the candidate for election officials resides. The draft introduces a provision that clearly indicates that providing service and working conditions for the proper performance of tasks by electoral officials is a task entrusted to the municipality, i.e. municipalities will receive the funds from the state budget. The amendment imposes new tasks on the State Election Commission to maintain the list of persons for whom the court has finally ruled that the passive electoral right has been lost in connection with the lodging of an incorrect illustrative statement and to make available data on those persons to the relevant electoral committees registering candidates in elections, i.e. the so-called. Lies of illustration. The President of the Court will be obliged to forward immediately the State Election Commission's final ruling to the court stating that a mirrored person has made an incorrect illustrative statement. The implementation of these tasks will contribute to the proper and efficient conduct of the procedure for registering candidates in elections. A provision is also proposed to ensure that there are no general elections, to the authorities of the municipalities' auxiliary bodies and to the lower-ranking bodies, and to referenda to the local authorities. An exception to the above rule will be the elections to the district councils of the M.S. Warsaw. As we know, there is a separate electoral procedure in the area of M.S. Warsaw. In addition, it will be possible to conduct these elections, including supplementary elections to the Senate, elections to the authorities of local self-government units: early, complementary, renewed and to the new council or early elections. As far as the effects of the proposed law are concerned, the project will certainly have positive socio-economic effects. The project has no financial implications for the budgets of local government units. At the same time, it will bring benefits to the state budget in connection with, among others. a derogation from the need to bear costs for the equipment of the electoral premises with equipment for recording images and sound and to shorten the judicial and administrative procedure in cases of complaints against the resolution of the State Election Commission on the division of the electoral district. I will remind you that here the time limit for the person concerned is three days, and the court also has five days to investigate the case after such an action has been brought before the Supreme Administrative Court. [[Voice from the room: Where is the legislation?]] It is proposed that the law should enter into force 7 days after the date of publication, with the exception of amendments to the Article. 420 The electoral code, which is intended for applicants to enter into force on 1 January 2019. The designation of a shorter than 14 days vacathio legis results from the need to ensure that the provisions on the summary procedure for dealing with complaints on the resolutions of the State Election Commission on the division into electoral districts are implemented as soon as possible. This involves ensuring a smooth settlement of pending proceedings by the Supreme Administrative Court. Since the proposed amendments do not conflict with the principle of a democratic rule of law and the need for such amendments is extremely urgent, it is proposed that the law should enter into force 7 days after the date of publication. These provisions are not covered by European Union law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u39	Please take the floor of Mr Ireneus Profit, Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u40	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Commission proposal for a law amending the electoral code law and certain other laws. The draft law submitted was submitted by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications in order to comply with the requests made by the State Election Commission. The proposed amendments are aimed at correcting, supplementing or derogating the provisions introduced in the electoral code by the Act of 11 January 2018. We made a number of comments and reservations at the time, but they were not accepted by the High House. The current bill is partly trying to correct the shortcomings of the regulation adopted at that time. The practice of the new electoral legislation introduced at the beginning of the year and the issue of ensuring compliance with EU legislation, in particular the famous Regulation No 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016, briefly referred to as GDPR, will undoubtedly affect the need for a smooth procedure and the adoption of this amendment. Its essence comes down to three fundamental issues. Firstly, the novel envisages changes in the procedure for the appointment of electoral officials. The project extends the catalogue of people who can perform this function. In addition to the staff of offices serving government and local authorities or their subordinates or supervised by them, electoral officials may also be other persons with at least 5 years' experience in these offices or their units. In addition, in the event of a threat concerning the performance of tasks related to ensuring the smooth functioning of the regional electoral committees, the Head of the National Election Office will be able to entrust, no longer than for the time of the elections concerned, the performance of the function of an electoral officer to a person who does not meet the requirements of residence and place of work. The introduction of these provisions will undoubtedly allow the head of the CWB to be more flexible in the appointment of electoral officials whose shoulders will be burdened with the proper conduct of the electoral procedure in each municipality. Secondly, the proposal provides for a shortening of the judicial and administrative procedure for complaints against the resolutions of the State Election Commission concerning the division of the electoral district. As proposed by the applicants, these actions will only be dealt with by the Supreme Administrative Court of the three Judges, and thus one instance instead of the two existing courts. Due to the very tight electoral calendar, the speed of such proceedings should be a priority. The change should therefore be assessed positively. The third major amendment is the repeal of the Act of 11 January this year. rules on the transmission and recording of the work of the Regional Election Commissions. General Data Protection Officer in his position of April this year. which responds to the request of the State Election Commission clearly indicates that without questioning the right to public information resulting from the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, it should be noted that the solutions contained in the electoral code require compliance with the provisions on the protection of personal data, in particular the GDPR Regulation. It points out in its conclusions that the extent of the adjustments and the number and severity of risks in practice may prevent lawful data protection of personal data from being transmitted during elections. I'm quoting PKW. In this context, although, as a parliamentary circle, we consider the idea of broadcasting from electoral premises to be correct for the transparency of the electoral process, we consider the committee's proposal to repeal the rules in this regard as reasonable, but it is important to return to this subject and in peace, without the pressure attached to the election deadline, to prepare rules on the transmission of elections which will comply with the provisions of the GDPR Regulation. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle requests that the bill be submitted for further legislative work. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u78	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth contained in the Parliamentary Printing No. 2563 on the Government's draft Law on Higher Education and Science, print No. 2446. The Sejm at its 62nd meeting on 9 May 2018 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth for consideration. The Committee on Education, Science and Youth, after considering this draft law at its meetings on 24, 29 and 30 May 2018, brings: The High Sejm will approve the attached draft law. The Commission shall act in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 The Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall submit 90 minority applications at the request of the applicants. The Act merges the legislation on higher education and science, scattered today in numerous other laws. It regulates the way universities operate, their structure, their authority, the way students conduct their studies, the rights and obligations of students, the functioning of student authorities and student organisations, as well as issues relating to university employees, scientific activities, the way they conduct postgraduate studies and specialized training. The Act introduces federations of universities. Thanks to the synergy effect and the evaluation criterion, universities forming federations can gain a higher position in rankings. The Act regulates the award of degrees and titles in higher education and science, the training of PhD students and the evaluation of doctoral schools, as well as the evaluation of the quality of scientific activities. It also defines the disciplinary responsibility of academic teachers, students and PhD students. It regulates the situation of foreigners at Polish universities. It regulates the functioning of representative institutions of higher education institutions such as the Main Council of Science and Higher Education, the Conference of Rectors of Academic Schools of Poland, the Conference of Public Rectors of Professional Universities, the Conference of Vocational Rectors of Polish Schools and others. It deals with IT systems operating at universities, including the protection of personal data processed by these systems. It sets out how to award scholarships and prizes. It regulates the financing of universities and universities and the conduct of the university's financial management. The bill aroused turbulent discussions in the academic community, which were also reflected in the speeches of our committee. Many opinions, often contradictory, have been reported. This was due to various visions of the functioning of universities. Hence the high number of minority proposals. In particular, the differences in the evaluation were presented on the establishment of a university board, its composition and competences, changes in the competence of rectors and senates, the possibility of changing the structure of the university consisting in the liquidation of departments. There were also concerns as to whether financial resources would be provided for effective implementation of higher education reform in order to improve the quality of education and scientific work in real terms. This is also the issue of minority proposals. Attention was paid to the need to guarantee adequate remuneration in institutes of the Polish Academy of Sciences. The introduction of doctoral scholarships as a universal solution was particularly welcomed. The problems of humanities were recognised when assessing the categories of universities. Some of the objections raised concerned a draft subject to public discussion before the printing was sent to the Sejm - some of them were corrected before the draft law was passed to the Sejm. During the examination of the draft, the committee adopted around 100 amendments. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Prime Minister! I have the honour to present also the report of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth, print No. 2564, on the governmental draft law Rules introducing the Law on Higher Education and Science, print No. 2447. The Sejm at its 62nd meeting on 9 May 2018, pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth for consideration. The Committee on Education, Science and Youth, after considering this draft law at its meetings on 24 and 30 May 2018, brings: The High Sejm will approve the attached draft law. The Commission shall, in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm shall, at the request of the applicant, submit two minority applications. High Seym! This bill didn't wake up. so many doubts. Several clarification amendments were adopted. The bill contains a set of legal changes necessary in other laws due to the introduction of new solutions by the Law on Higher Education, print No. 2563. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u80	Dear Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Government's draft Law on Higher Education and Science, print No 2446, and the Government's draft Law Rules introducing the Law on Higher Education and Science, print No 2447. The proposals aim at consolidating, standardising and simplifying basic legislation in the field of higher education and science. The provisions laid down in the various laws so far will be contained in a single legal act. The draft Law Law introducing the Law on Higher Education and Science is a consequence of new regulations dictated by effective and efficient implementation. The reform of science and higher education is an answer to the need to improve the quality of education and improve the quality of research in Poland. It was developed with the participation of the academic community, through extensive consultations and arrangements. The reform changes the approach to science - instead of focusing on the number of studies carried out and the number of publications awarded will be their quality. Higher levels of research work will result in a dynamic development of science and innovation of the economy. Projects include: improving the quality of education in studies, increasing the salaries of academic teachers, abolishing the obligation to habilitation while proposing a package for obtaining this degree of scientific autonomy, as well as new solutions for promoting doctors and reviewing doctoral discourses. The submission introduces a system of broad support for regional universities. It should be noted that the solutions adopted were supported by the stakeholders themselves - regional universities from all over Poland. The projects also offer tools to raise the position of Polish universities in international rankings. Federations of universities, programmes of research universities or support for regional initiatives are only a few of the solutions proposed in the law. A new model of doctoral training is introduced - doctoral schools, which will be carried out on a stationary basis. Each PhD student will be provided with a doctoral scholarship and parental leave, and the PhD students will receive an increased scholarship. The proposed projects include changes in the area of: university system, evaluation of science and competence to award degrees, solutions for students and employees of universities, as well as new solutions for regional universities, with an algorithm that takes into account the needs of the regions and the programme of the Regional Initiative of Excellence - and so more than PLN 100 million per year will go to regional centres conducting high-level research in particular disciplines, and each region will receive a guaranteed financial envelope. The Act also provides for the settlement of the academic environment with the period of the Polish People's Republic. Decommunization of Polish universities is a long-awaited issue. After the end of World War II, universities were not neutral, strictly reserved for the science of the field of activity, and became an important element of the communism and vassalization of the Polish state. Higher education requires a moral rebirth in this area. According to the provisions of the project, persons who worked in or cooperated with the security bodies of the Polish People's Republic will not be allowed to sit in the institutions of universities and representative institutions. Science and higher education obligations and their impact on Poland's economic and social development have a detailed description in the Responsible Development Strategy by 2020. The strategy for the development of Polish science and higher education is a key element of the plan of responsible economic development of Poland presented by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki. High Seym! There were different voices and opinions of higher education and science environments with regard to the proposals submitted. We listened very carefully, which resulted in a large number of amendments proposed and adopted in the course of the work. It's a proof of going out to meet the demands of university representatives, academics, students and PhD students. As the broad dialogue continues, I submit to the High House a package of additional amendments. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice advocates the adoption of proposed laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I will now refer one at a time to your unfortunate accusations. As far as Eastern Poland is concerned, here several Members have asked about expenditure and growth. Lubelska University of Technology - almost 5% increase in grants this year. Impossible, isn't it? Maria Curie-Skłodowska University - also growth, Rzeszowski University - also growth. Do these figures confirm your thesis? After all, this is the supposedly wrong algorithm we've introduced, and the numbers are that these universities are gaining money. Not to mention, that special funds, in the amount of nearly PLN 1 billion, were directed from the National Centre for Research and Development. These are facts, hard numbers. As far as regional universities are concerned, there have been a lot of accusations here that are false. Perhaps some Members would like higher education to develop in this way. We want sustainable development and equal opportunities for universities, but on one condition - that they will want to improve the quality of scientific research and the quality of education, because the concern of all of us, the High House, is that universities in Poland should develop and not be stagnated. Besides, the representatives of the Parliament of Polish Students who support this law and are looking at you in advance probably feel the same way in this regard. With regard to regional universities and financial mechanisms, because this is an important concern that emerged today during the debate, first of all, the state-owned professional universities will have a separate financial algorithm, guaranteed funding. 35 state universities, vocational universities have a separate financial algorithm, they will be assessed separately, so they can develop in these very important smaller districts, because they have a very important role. This year 28 of these universities are gaining on the algorithm. In previous years, unfortunately, much less was gained by the financial algorithm that was previously in force. More facts, ladies and gentlemen. As far as regional universities are concerned, going further, large universities, research universities, they will be financed differently, and regional universities, these middle schools, otherwise, will have different rules in the financial algorithm. Of course, you do not want to talk about it, and we have repeatedly said that they will race in their groups so that they can use their strengths in developing research and the quality of education. For the first time after 1989, we devote a separate financial stream to the development of regional universities, from which research universities will be excluded. The largest 15 universities will be excluded from the special financial stream, because it was intended for regional universities to have guaranteed funding for development which they had not previously had. As regards the increase in spending on higher education and science, in the legislation that is currently in force, in the legislation that follows the Civic Platform, the increase in spending on higher education and science would amount to PLN 100 million each year. Such guarantee mechanisms have been left to us in succession in the Law on Higher Education and Science and in several other laws. Now this mechanism will on average increase the financial resources by PLN 1 billion, 700 million next year, in the following years up to 1300 million, not counting the additional funds that will appear in each budget law, which has been won in addition. Thus, for the first time, the financial growth mechanism is being introduced by us and not by the people of the opposition. That's another thing. With regard to the allegations of humanities and research in this area, for the first time humanities will be judged primarily in their own disciplines, alone and not between different departments. Departments are now very diverse, which makes it often compared e.g. social sciences with humanities. By force, it's hard to compare. publications from psychology with publications from pedagogy. Now each of these disciplines will be compared in their own disciplines. This will be fair comparison within scientific disciplines, not within different departments. What's that gonna do? Separate sources of funding for humanities. As for the doubts that arise, whether the Minister will be able to eliminate the course of his studies if he so wishes, because that is how it sounded in the statements of some Members - no, he will not have the opportunity to do so. The bill forbids him to do so. It can only close the course of study if the Polish Accreditation Board negatively evaluates such course of study or if there is no recruitment for two years. Only in situations like this. I will tell you more, we are expanding the autonomy of universities in the development of new courses of study, because universities that will have category A+, category A and category B+ will be able to establish without the Minister's consent the directions they like in these disciplines, even though they may not like it at some point. Well, that's real autonomy. We expand university autonomy in this area. With this move, I would like to move on to the question of the university system and to the amendments that we tabled as a parliamentary club at second reading, because this is very important. [[Are you the envoy?]] Yes, I am of Law and Justice, and I am proud of it, sir. As regards the notification of changes in matters relating to the choice of university authorities, with regard to university autonomy, firstly, the autonomy of the university in this law is increased. Mr Nykiel, former Rector, said that autonomy is an opportunity to decide for itself. Autonomy is not independence. Note that this is not the same. So what will it look like? The university board will mostly be able to consist of people from within. The entire university council will be elected by the Senate. One representative will be from the student council. In its entirety, the academic community chooses the university board. After these changes, the University Council will be able to comment on most of the things, but will not have a decisive opinion either on the strategy of the university or on the statute or on the choice of the rector. The Rector will elect a college of electors, i.e. a meeting elected in democratic elections from all academic groups. They will be able to submit candidates for the rector to the university board and other bodies indicated in the university statute, as it is now. Issues related to the statute of the university. It's a constitution for college. Who's adopting this charter? The Senate of the University. Who chooses the senate of the university? Academic community. What is the risk to university autonomy here? These are populist allegations, because the autonomy of the university in this area is extended. Some may be afraid of it, but we, as a government of law and justice, want to trust universities. Let them be realized in the manner in which the academic community considers it right in its autonomy. Further things to do with this bill. I would like to make it clear that if you vote against this law, you will deprive future generations of doctoral candidates for compulsory doctoral scholarships. The vote against this law is to block the universal scholarship system for future PhD students, for future elites of Polish science. That's one thing. Allow universities to continue to regulate their fees in the way they like to increase student fees and deceive students. This bill makes college fees... [[Colleges don't cheat students.]] Colleges don't cheat students? Then ask your college buddies or student council in Szczecin if this happens. [[You're the minister of science, and you say universities cheat students?]] Yeah, unfortunately, it happens that universities cheat students, which is why we're gonna block it. By voting against this law, you will continue to allow universities to raise their fees in an uncontrolled way. [[You're very young and confident.]] [[Don't judge who's gonna think. This is every man's business.]] I'm just saying what you'll be for if you vote against it. As regards retirement age, first, the retirement age is governed by general pension rules. We in the Law on Higher Education do not touch the Act on Pensions at all. The pension system, which applies to all citizens, i.e. the general pension system, is also in force in this law. That's one thing. Second thing. No one can force him to retire in accordance with the current rules, and that is not an option. Retiring is a right, not a duty. I can't imagine forcing anyone to retire in any way. The retirement age is classic, it is from a law that is universally applicable, in no way discriminates in this case the sexes. I will tell you more, the age of running for individual bodies at universities is for both sexes at the same level - 67 years. Therefore, there is no distinction between sexes in terms of functioning within the academy, within the university. Therefore, this claim also appears to be incorrect. As far as the issues of the president's competence are concerned, let me remind you that he is elected by the electoral college in democratic elections. If the academic community entrusts this function to a member of its academic community, I hope that it has confidence in it. This is how a joint academic is realized. State higher vocational schools. I have already mentioned the issue of the financial algorithm here, but I would just like to remind you that the Conference of Rectors of State Higher Professional Schools has explicitly supported this law. For two reasons - we have for them a separate financial stream and a dedicated stream of excellence for the good education of our students, and we are also raising the rank of these universities even from the point of view of naming, because they will now be called public professional universities, not vocational schools. It was often wrong. The environment of these professional universities has asked us to raise this rank on a semantic level despite all this. This is one of the things that goes to the university, to state vocational schools. As regards issues related to the powers to award degrees, they will depend on the scientific categories to be awarded on the basis of an evaluation of the scientific acquis. Ladies and gentlemen, there are three different kinds of criteria. The first criterion is purely scientific. We point out how publications look in a given discipline at a given university, i.e. it is a matter of publishing in magazines and publications. The second point is the question of cooperation with industry. This is particularly important in technical sciences. The question is whether there are certain numbers of patents, whether they cooperate with outside companies and what resources they obtain. And the third criterion, which is very important in terms of these allegations concerning humanities, concerns the socio-economic impact. If humanists, social sciences have a socio-economic impact on the activities they carry out, they will receive additional profits for this, because the scientific category translates into financial gains and thus will have the powers to award degrees. This is very important because this third criterion is particularly important for regional universities. They play an extremely important role in social and cultural matters. In the case of this third criterion they will be able to prove themselves. This third criterion in addition will be more important in social sciences and humanities. For social sciences and humanities too, we provide for a separate procedure, which has already been voted out in committee, an evaluation of scientific activities. It will also be an expert activity, and not only will it be an assessment on a parametric basis, i.e. merely quotibility or publication in specific magazines. So we are introducing an expert activity in the assessment of this acquis, and this is also an important thing from the point of view of the development of monographs and other scientific publications among humanists. As regards the allegation of public consultation and amendments, these amendments do not fundamentally change this proposal. They're opening up to the academic community. We presented the first draft law in September last year, in January we presented the next draft after the first round of amendments, in April we presented the next round of amendments. We have now tabled amendments at the session of the Parliamentary Committee, which were still due to the voice of the academic community, and now we are presenting their last round. If this is an objection to the method of consultation that within nine months we have made four tours of amendments to the bill, then I do not know what charge we would have been attacked if we had not taken these amendments into account? Because it's the opening-up of the academic community, these are the amendments that we're making that come out... [[Or Terletsky.]] ...from students', academic, doctoral, senior scientific and junior backgrounds. These environments are very diverse and these are the voices we take into account. As far as the opportunities ahead of us are concerned, the position of Poland in international rankings is such an opportunity, here you have also mentioned federations, federations of universities, which operate in many countries around the world. And one example. The University of Warsaw is now at 314th position in the Shanghai ranking. If he entered the Federation with the Warsaw Medical University he would be 100 places higher. This is the negligence that we have done over the last few years, because the academic community is spread out and we do not exploit the common potential of these universities. But I would point out that these are federations. These universities continue to become separate universities, but together they boast about their achievements in the international arena. This is also an opportunity for regional universities that can enter the federations, while maintaining the autonomy of individual universities, but thus gaining higher scientific categories, higher financial resources and greater powers to give degrees. This is an opportunity to develop potential in the regions. Finally, I would also like to talk about financial issues in the coming years. First of all, we have raised minimum salaries since January 2019, in particular assistants and assistants, which will increase by 30%, on average by PLN 800 as regards the minimum salary. But back to the committee report, I would like to say that indeed, as the President said, there was a rather interesting discussion at the committee meeting. One of the issues raised was the issue of the period during which the medal will be broadcast. The project provides that it can be granted in the years 2018 to 2021. This period is intended to allow for various celebrations related to regaining independence and forming borders of the Second Republic of Poland. And that's a very good assumption. On the other hand, in the case of Silesia, a mistake was made in my opinion in this law. It is worth recalling that in 1921 the plebiscite took place in Silesia and the Third Silesian Uprising broke out, which actually directly influenced the determination of the borders of the Silesian area, which was later granted to Poland, while Silesia was only joined to Poland a year later. On June 15, 1922, the intersocious committee called on Poland and Germany to take part in the disputed area and on June 17, Katowice left the French garrison. In fact, there was no Polish administration in Silesia until June 1922. Only on June 20, 1922, the Polish Army entered Silesia and then the border posts abandoned by the French were filled by the Silesian insurgents. Only after 20 June, the following cities were included by the Polish administration, the army entered them, the process of construction of the Polish administration continued, and the symbolic act of Poland's accession to Upper Silesia was signed in Katowice on 16 July 1922. We in Silesia celebrate of course the anniversary of the next Silesian uprisings, while we also celebrate solemnly all events connected with commemorating Silesia's connection to Poland just in 1922, especially since it was then, in 1922, during these celebrations that were publicly and often very beautifully thanked those people who contributed to it through their work, through participation in Silesian uprisings, but also work for Polish consciousness in Silesia and led to the fact that these uprisings could succeed and part of Upper Silesia was attached to Poland. Therefore, we make an amendment extending the duration of this award until 2022 so that the Medal of the Centennial of Recovered Independence can actually be awarded during the celebrations which will commemorate the real events connected with the accession of Silesia to Poland, and these events will be commemorated in their century, i.e. in 2022. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u224	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, I have the honour to present to the High House a report of the committee on the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Act on the Social Dialogue Council and other institutions of social dialogue, print No 2281. On 27 February 2018, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law from the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs to the first reading. On 6 and 7 June 2018, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy carried out its first reading and examined the project. The committee's report is in print 2572. The proposal submitted by the President of the Republic of Poland to amend the Act on the Council of Social Dialogue and other institutions of social dialogue is the result of an evaluation of the functioning of the provisions of the Act of 24 July 2015 on the Council of Social Dialogue and other institutions of social dialogue, and then presented to the President of the Republic of Poland a recommendation for changes. The draft amendment contains proposals to improve social dialogue at both national and regional level. In particular, provision should be made for: to extend to draft strategies and plans for a catalogue of documents to be consulted by the social side of the Council; to grant the opportunity to speak to the Minister responsible for affairs. public finances with a request for a general interpretation on the application of tax law; allowing the adoption of resolutions of the party of employees and employers of the council by means of a correspondence vote; participation in the work of the council, with an advisory voice, a representative of the chief labour inspector; clarification of the competences of the voivodship councils for social dialogue and increased resources for the functioning of the voivodship councils for social dialogue. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed regulation will have a positive impact on social and economic relations due to the effect of improving the functioning of the forum for tripartite cooperation and dialogue between the workers' side, the employers' side and the government side of the Council of Social Dialogue, and at regional level the regional social dialogue board. In view of the positive effects of the proposed amendments, I propose that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft law together with the amendments. Thank you very much. I quote these words because they show the fundamental difference between how social dialogue is treated in Europe, and how it is - unfortunately otherwise - in Poland. The Tripartite Commission - social dialogue in this regulated formula draws its origins in Poland from the 1993 State Enterprise Pact. In Poland, the concept of social dialogue is often confused and applied interchangeably with civil dialogue. These are two completely different concepts. As far as social dialogue is concerned, here are trade unions, employers' organisations and, often, government parties. Until 2001, until July, this social dialogue in the Tripartite Commission was based on the principles set out in the Council of Ministers Regulation. Only in July 2001 did the Act on the Tripartite Commission appear. She was very lame, too. She functioned differently. In fact, it was such a very crisis moment in December 2008, when Prime Minister Donald Tusk at the meeting of the Bureau of the Tripartite Commission in the face of a crisis in Europe, in America, was asked mainly by employers, what we are doing in Poland, to answer... he was not supposed to answer, he just answered, I heard it with my own ears: If you don't know what to do, it's best not to do anything. Desperate employers have asked us, the unions, to enter into bilateral negotiations. The result was so-called. The anti-crisis package, which the government, Prime Minister Tusk, has committed to employers, is taking everything or nothing. Such declarations were made publicly in front of the dialogue centre. Unfortunately, this was not kept, and soon it began to break. It showed how fragile and bad it was, it showed that not only regulation but, above all, good will of the parties is needed for dialogue. I think that such a complete breakdown of social dialogue in Poland was the pushing up of the retirement age against the attitude of trade unions, contrary to social protests. That situation practically led to social dialogue and to the cessation, cessation of activities by the Tripartite Commission for Economic and Social Affairs. For almost two years, we had a situation where it did not actually function, but it was not a waste of time, because at the time, at the initiative of "Solidarity," the social partners - representative trade union organisations and employers - worked together, negotiated a new bill on the Council of Social Dialogue and this project - in the previous term, because in July 2015, 24 July - was adopted and adopted. The actual start and resumption of work in the Social Dialogue Council has already taken place during this term of office. Today we can actually say that social dialogue in Poland has been resurrected. These most important features, the provisions of this new law are: rotating leadership in the Council - at the moment not only the Minister of Labour, possibly the Prime Minister, but also, rotationally, individual social partners have written down this lead; problem teams - there are dozens of problem teams; better empowerment and better functioning of voivodship social dialogue councils. It also provides that within 24 months of the date of entry into force of the Social Dialogue Council Act, the partners shall review the provisions of the Act and, when assessing its functioning, propose amendments. That's what happened. The social partners asked the President of the Republic of Poland to be the father of this law. Draft amendments to the Act based on proposals submitted by the Social Dialogue Council were prepared by the President of the Republic of Poland. Law and Justice fully supports this bill [[♪ The bell ♪]] and will vote in favour of it. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u244	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, I present a report on the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Law on Free Legal Assistance and Legal Education and certain other laws, prints Nos 1868 and 2616. The purpose of the presentation, as the President points out, is above all to extend the scope of the subject matter and the law in question, to improve the regulation of the law on the identification of non-governmental organisations which provide legal assistance - further - to enable these organisations to provide free civic guidance and to link free legal assistance and free civic advice with legal assistance provided at the stage of judicial proceedings. The Sejm at its 49th meeting of 11 October 2017 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred this proposal to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and to the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy for consideration. The Commission's meetings on the project were held on 25 October and 7 June 2018. An extraordinary subcommittee was established for the detailed examination of the project, which I had the privilege of chairing. The subcommittee was held on 9 November 2017 and 30 May 2018. Written opinions for the project were presented by: the Union of Polish Metropolitans, the National Chamber of Legal Counsels, the Supreme Bar Council, the Attorney General, as well as the Foundation of University Legal Advice. The meetings of the committee and subcommittee were attended by representatives of the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland with the Minister of Surówka-Pasek, officials of the Ministry of Justice with Minister Marcin Warchoł, employees of the Ministry of Family, representatives of the National Council of the Judicial Council, the National Chamber of Legal Counsels, as well as numerous representatives of social organizations, in particular the Foundation of University Legal Advices, the Association of Social Information Centres - CIS, the Association of OVUM, the Association of Self-Governmental Social Assistance Centres, the Foundation of Academia Iuris and the Office of Civil Advice. At the subcommittee meetings and then the committees, all the regulations were systematically discussed. A number of substantive amendments, including two such amendments, were adopted at the last committee meeting. As part of the amendments tabled - this, I think, should be highlighted in particular - a catalogue of entities entitled to receive free legal assistance, mediation and free advice has been opened. Corrective amendments and legislative amendments have been tabled. There were a lot of them. 12 Members voted in favour of the bill at the joint committee meeting last week, 3 against, 6 abstentions. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, after considering the draft law, invite the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law, as proposed in the report of the Committee on Printing No 2616. I would like to thank you for your constructive cooperation with the Members. I would very much like to thank the Minister and all excellent colleagues from the Office of Law and Government of the President's Office, as well as representatives of the Ministry of Justice, in particular Mr Marcin Romanowski from the Institute and Mrs Aleksandr Różycka. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u246	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! PiS naturally supports this project. The idea of Andrzej Duda from the 2015 campaign to create a mechanism of free legal assistance for citizens was undoubtedly a jackpot, because it responded to widespread social expectations for such assistance to arise. However, the execution by the PO-PSL coalition was, in colloquially speaking, poor... [[Voice from the room: And what's that for?]] ...also because they acted without conviction, they acted hurriedly and on the eve of the election. The point is that there were rules that few could actually benefit from. Many points of legal aid in Poland were lit and still glowing with emptyness. By adopting the amendment, we want to change that. We assume that we will not substantially increase the cost of the system, but we certainly want to increase its efficiency in order to use the public funds allocated to it in an efficient, optimal way. Incomparably more citizens will be able to benefit from the aid, as it is enough to state that they are unable to bear its costs. We assume that, in principle, those who are in need will benefit. On the other hand, the existing or even initially proposed complex directory and the system of verification of allowances have made this system not only less efficient but also less friendly to citizens. Public opinion polls show that bureaucracy and lack of trust are among the fundamental problems of the wider justice system. That is why we have sought solutions under this law in order to limit such perception, to limit the field to such allegations. Our aim was to increase the number of legal aid beneficiaries, to re-bureaucratise the system and make it as simple as possible. It should also be added that the scope of the aid has been greatly extended, which is very strongly supported by the Law and Justice, as this aid will not include, as so far, only legal advice itself, which has often been complained about, but also, which in turn was expected by many citizens, the preparation of basic pleadings. In addition, we create new services: free civic guidance and free mediation. We also emphasize and develop the original intuition so that this system also includes legal education. Advice will be provided on important matters of life, which are not necessarily strictly legal, i.e. for the indebted, in housing and social matters. It is worth emphasising the possibility of mediation. We all know that courts are flooded with a lot of cases, which means that, even in a simple case, you often have to wait for months or even years. The development of alternative means of resolving disputes would help to unblock courts and, no less importantly, help to create better social relations. We know very well that not all disputes have to end in court. In conclusion, the Law and Justice Club is in favour of the project. We support the creation of a system of free assistance, a wide, accessible, citizen-friendly system. On this occasion, I would also like to make a few amendments, put them in your hands, Marshal. In order to avoid interpretation doubts, which were signalled during the committee's work, inter alia. We want to clarify the rules so that there is no doubt that free civic guidance under this law does not affect the functioning of such advice provided or operated outside the system. Come on. We would like to clarify the provisions setting out requirements for those providing free civic guidance, including the requirement to have a higher education. These are the most important amendments. There's five of them in total. I'm handing them over to your marshal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u252	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In this way, the weakest, financially deprived people receive instruments that enable them to safeguard their rights in a situation of dispute with the state or an unfair corporation. I am therefore pleased that the Sejm of the previous term passed a law on free legal aid and legal education. However, it should be noted that the solutions adopted require in-depth analysis and significant improvements, as best demonstrated by the latest report of the Supreme Audit Chamber. One could hope that the presidential bill would be an opportunity for positive changes in the issue of free legal assistance. Unfortunately, despite the undoubtedly good intentions of the President, the project contains numerous legal shortcomings and its effectiveness is questionable. I presented my reservations about this project during last year's debate. Most problematic entries were removed from the project during the work of the Emergency Sub-Committee, but the availability of free mediation may be limited due to the fact that an entry on lists of permanent mediators by district courts is required. Let us remember still a small number of mediators who, moreover, usually operate in larger urban centres, as well as a small number of lawyers and legal advisers in the number of mediators listed by the Presidents of the District Courts. The risks arising from the introduction of specialization of on-call services at legal aid points are also perceived. There are indications that a large proportion of visits to such places have covered more than one area of law, e.g. family, housing, tax and criminal law. In the current version, the project continues to contain an incomprehensible and even harmful record of the creation by the voivod of a list of non-governmental organisations entitled to provide free legal assistance. This list is unnecessary, as the verification of the organisation will take place each time the seniors perform the competitions. This is another unnecessary manifestation of the centralization of the state. In addition, in practice this can lead to responsibility shifts. Self-governments may mistakenly assume that the voivode has made a sufficient assessment of the organisation. In addition, Minister, please note that the governor is entitled by law to cancel the contract, terminate the contract for free legal assistance in the event of a breach of the rules governing the inclusion of certain organisations in the list of voivodships, so the voivodeship will maintain for its part an official apparatus which will qualify certain organisations to be included in the list of voivodeships, and the voivodship will in turn maintain a camera that will verify whether the continuing organisation with which the contract is concluded fulfils the conditions for listing organisations authorised to provide such assistance. Isn't it worthwhile, instead of maintaining unnecessary official mass both in the county government and at the state level, in the office of the voivodeship, to allocate these funds to the most deprived for legal assistance and civic guidance? Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u274	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! When referring to the comments made here, first of all, I would like to refer to all the proposals relating to the list of voivodships. The creation of a list of voivodships as a stage of indirect verification of persons, or otherwise: entities, not so much persons as organisations entitled to provide civic guidance, is, in effect, the implementation of a request which has been made by the Supreme Audit Chamber to harmonise the way in which legal assistance is provided free of charge and to create a verification mechanism. So far, it has appeared that individual elders have decided on their own when and under what circumstances they consider that the social organisation fulfils the conditions necessary to provide free legal assistance, while the verification of the voivodeship will be purely formal, it is a certain standardization of those elements which the organisation must fulfil in order to qualify for this system. On the other hand, this initial qualification will relieve the old men, because they will no longer be obliged to carry out a review of whether the organisation fulfils all the formal conditions for the granting of guarantees, such as the secrecy of certain information, ethical behaviour or internal control within this organisation, which will provide services in the field of free legal assistance or free civic advice, while only the burden associated with the choice of the organisation with which it will sign will be passed on to the elder. It should also be noted that it is through the voivodeship that funds are transferred to provide free legal assistance for the implementation of this system. Moreover, as regards the position of the elders, it is important to bear in mind that they are carrying out the task assigned to the government administration, therefore maintaining this contact with the voivode as a representative of the government administration in the field seems reasonable, but from the point of view of the control of public spending. Another issue concerns training or the conditions that citizens' advisers should fulfil. In addition to the condition of training, which was proposed in the submission, there is another requirement, namely to have appropriate experience in providing such services. The law or the submission requires that such an organisation, which will compete in a competition for civic advice, have at least 2 years of experience in this field. Therefore, the 70-hour course is one of the elements, but not the only element that needs to be met in order to be qualified for this type of civic advice. Another issue raised concerns family matters which have been dealt with in previous years by points of free legal assistance. According to the statistics made available by the Ministry of Justice, family law cases accounted for 7.92% all the advice given, while those concerning divorce, separation, distribution of common assets accounted for 5.8% all advice provided under the free legal aid scheme. Currently there are 1524 points of free legal aid. From the part of the Ministry of Justice, 95 million PLN per year is allocated for this purpose. As regards matters relating to the qualifications of mediators, I would like to refer to the regulation currently in force, Article. 183 Code of Civil Procedure. This regulation, which is included in the submission, in the draft law, corresponds to the requirements which are foreseen for the mediator under the Code of Civil Procedure. In a commentary on this part of the Code, one can quote the words of the author, which indicates that the mediator's qualification for mediation is not so much his education as a certain actual ability to mediate. These issues were also raised by the environment of lawyers and legal advisers with whom this submission was consulted. They pointed out that no counsel or lawyer could be forced to mediate because not everyone had personality traits that would allow him to mediate. On the other hand, as regards the issue of the system's capacity, the legal advisers themselves indicated in the opinion addressed to the High House that at this point it was not possible to assess the efficiency of the system, but it could be pointed out - and this was also mentioned in the justification for the translation - that at present most of the measures which could be used as free legal aid are not used because of the closed, very limited catalogue of eligible persons. The Presidency's submission serves to make better use of the budget resources that will be used for this purpose. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u280	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law does not change the scope of the entitled persons and does not interfere with their rights to benefits so far, while giving them new rights. According to the project, people deported to forced labour and imprisoned in labour camps by the Third Reich and the Soviet Union during and after World War II will be given the right of priority to environmental social protection, including to obtain a place in a social care home, to benefit from health care and pharmaceutical services outside the order. In addition, the Act will allow local authorities to provide assistance to eligible persons through communication facilities, housing, cultural, health and education services. In accordance with this project, the persons fulfilling the conditions listed in the Act will be issued the ID cards of the person deported to forced labour or imprisoned in the labour camp by the Third Reich and the USSR. The proposed changes are supported by the Union of Combatants of the Republic of Poland and the Former Political Prisoners and the Union of Invalids of the War of the Republic of Poland. The powers conferred by the law are similar to those currently available to combatants, victims of repression and anti-communist opposition activists. Persons deported to forced labour and imprisoned in labour camps by the Third Reich and the USSR due to age and health require increased care and care for medical and social care. By introducing the changes contained in the project, access for persons entitled to the above mentioned. services will significantly improve. The project meets the expectations of victims of the occupation, as well as a sense of social justice. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u306	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law amending the law on trust services and electronic identification and some other laws, print No. 2502. The project entered the Sejm on 30 April 2018. The first reading took place at the meeting of the Sejm on 5 June and the project was then addressed to the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies. On the same day, a committee meeting was held on the subject. The main objectives of the proposed law are to provide Poles with access to e-services and public portals with a single login and password, to create a legal framework for the functioning of the national electronic identification scheme, to create legal bases for the provision and development by the Minister of Digitalization of the public mobile application. During the process of drafting the bill and the substantive discussion, MEPs tabled five amendments, which were accepted by the committee, concerning, among others, adding to the Act provisions which will ensure that the Internal Security Agency is able to examine whether the entity responsible for the system or entities carrying out the following activities in that system confirming the identity and verifying the identity of applicants for electronic identification, issuing electronic identification means or providing functionality allowing the authentication of persons to whom the electronic identification measure has been issued are not under the corporate control of foreign countries. The following amendment allows national banks and cooperative savings and credit banks to submit electronic applications and annexes to claims for benefits under the "Good start' scheme. It was proposed, in the form of an amendment, to change the provisions of the project in order to allow, in addition to the existing possibilities, the display of the Large Family Card also in the public mobile application following an agreement between ministers. In the course of the committee's work, new provisions were also voted to allow the entities mentioned in the proposed rules to use electronic identification means which contain data other than those identifying an officer or a person indicated in those provisions when performing the operational and diagnostic activities. In addition, 24 legislative and two substantive amendments were also tabled during committee work and were accepted by the committee. The Commission authorised the Legislative Office to submit editorial amendments. The Commission asks the High House to adopt the draft law together with the amendments. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u307	Thank you very much, sir. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u308	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The aim of the proposed law is, among other things, to provide each person with access to e-services in various public service portals using one login. The purpose of the Act is to enable the operation of online services for which the applicant will be able to consent to the automatic collection of data relating to him, from public registers kept by other public bodies and to the transmission of those data to the requested entity. This will make the use of electronic services more convenient and faster. The amendment also introduces regulations that will provide the legal basis for the functioning of the public mobile application. Within this application, the so-called operation will be started. mDocuments, i.e. electronic documents stored on mobile devices, the use of which will allow citizens, among others. to confirm the rights held, e.g. driver's licenses or other documents such as registration or ID. Public use of the mobile application will be free and voluntary. The proposed law also contains regulations concerning so-called. a cross-border hub that will allow integration of national service portals and identity confirmation mechanisms with similar portals and mechanisms in the European Union countries. In this way, Polish citizens will be able to use the administration services offered in other Member States, as well as, on the other hand, citizens of other European Union countries will be able to use the services of Polish administration. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed solutions are intended to promote socio-economic development. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u332	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The amendment to the law on the monitoring system for the carriage of goods by road aims to ensure a more effective control of the carriage of goods as indicated in the Act or Regulation of the Minister for Development and Finance as sensitive goods because of the vulnerability to tax law and fair competition rules. The solutions introduced by the amendment of the Law on the monitoring system for the carriage of goods by road alongside the recording of the transport of these sensitive goods to date will ensure the supervision of the transport by satellite systems. Data on the location of the means of transport, i.e. a specific date and time, i.e. geolocation data, and the locator number, will be obtained from locators and external location systems. The compilation of these data with the data from the notification document will allow the creation of a map of the carriage of goods and the identification of possible illegal carriages. The advantage of the solution proposed by the Ministry of Finance is that operators, mainly small and medium-sized enterprises, will not incur additional running costs because most carriers have means of transport equipped with data acquisition equipment, while those companies which do not have portable devices equipped with a satellite navigation module will be obliged to purchase such a device, but this is the cost within the limit of PLN 300, i.e. it is not a significant cost, and it will not apply to most small and medium-sized enterprises. The advantage of this solution implemented by the amendment of the Act under discussion is also the fact that if the litter does not have its own location systems, it will be able to use an additional application. The application is planned to be made available free of charge on the website of the Public Information Bulletin of the Ministry of Finance and on the Tax and Customs Electronic Services Platform. Entrepreneurs who do not have portable devices equipped with a satellite navigation module will be obliged to purchase such devices, as I mentioned. The costs of these devices will not exceed, as we know, 300 PLN once. The cost of providing a free application shall be covered by public funds. It is a cost of about 100,000. PLN and several million over the next 10 years to maintain the geolocation module. It will be expenditure of the state budget. Indeed, they should be as low as possible, but if we compare them with the benefits that will accompany them, it should be assumed that the benefits will cover these costs. They are estimated at over PLN 6 billion. These are estimates, but the estimates are quite promising. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u358	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the amendment of the Law of 18 December 2003 on plant protection, print No 2608. The aim of the amendment is to simplify and shorten administrative procedures, facilitate international trade in plant material, eliminate problems arising at border phytosanitary inspection of wood packaging materials, regulate the rules for cooperation between the State Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspection with the Agricultural and Food Quality Inspection and the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture in order to improve the effectiveness of this cooperation, introduce changes in the functioning of the integrated plant production system and lay down rules for keeping the register of exporters and eliminate interpretation doubts arising from the application of the Plant Protection Act of 18 December 2003. I will briefly present the proposed amendments. The amended act shall be amended to provide that the Provincial Plant Protection and Seed Inspector carries out official checks at the packing and sorting sites of plants, plant products or objects. It is proposed to simplify the procedure for entering entrepreneurs in the register by withdrawing the obligation to make an administrative decision. The entry will be made on the basis of a duly completed application. It is also proposed to refrain from carrying out checks on operators who do not carry out any activity in respect of cultivation, manufacture, storage, packaging, sorting, placing on the market or movement of plants, plant products or objects which are particularly susceptible to quarantine by quarantine organisms in a given year. It is also proposed that checks on operators entered in the register of exporters may be carried out by the State Plant Protection and Seeds Inspection or by bodies carrying out certification activities in integrated plant production on the basis of plant protection products rules. In order to prevent the spread of quarantine organisms, it is proposed that the Provincial Plant Protection and Seed Inspector may agree to carry out inspections of wooden packaging and wood used to immobilise or secure cargoes in a place other than the place where they were introduced into the territory of the Republic of Poland. The possibility of accepting destruction of wood packaging and wood used to immobilise or secure goods which have not been labelled in accordance with the standard is also permitted. The consequences of the changes envisaged shall be to take into account the tasks of the State Plant Protection and Seed Inspections to keep a register of exporters and to control the entities entered in the register. The proposed amendments to the amended law aim at horizontal regulation of the rules for cooperation between the State Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspections with the relevant services and inspections. The way in which data will be transmitted by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture to inspections on the eradication or prevention of the spread of organisms which are particularly dangerous to plants, which have not yet been present in our country, will be regulated in detail to monitor the regularity of the marketing and use of plant protection products in order to eliminate the risks to consumers or the environment associated with the incorrect use of these preparations, the control of the use of seed, including genetically modified seed. The proposed law will have a positive impact on entrepreneurs and entrepreneurship, including micro-entrepreneurs, because the changes introduced by the proposed law aim, among others. allowing access to new markets for Polish products of plant origin. The introduction of the proposed solutions may therefore result in an increase in trade in such goods. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. The Law and Justice Club requests that the bill submitted be passed. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applause]]

Adapting to these requirements is a basic condition for maintaining and acquiring new markets for Polish agri-food products. To this end, the provisions of the Act to date are supplemented by provisions concerning the subjecting of export entities to the supervision of the State Plant Protection and Seed Inspection and to the certification bodies of integrated plant production. These actions are dictated by the desire to guarantee the presence of Polish food in non-European markets, as well as to minimise the risk of events that have affected Poland's image and violated the opinion on the quality of food produced in our country. Exports of agri-food products are very important for Poland, so we should make every effort to ensure that our food sold on markets within and outside the Union is very good brand. Of course, international trade is important to us, but we must not ignore the fact that almost 80% of the European market goes to Europe. Polish agri-food products. It is pointed out that Polish food exports worldwide in 2017 amounted to EUR 27 billion, of which 21 brought exports to the EU countries. This is undoubtedly a key market for us. However, there are more and more threats that pose a threat to our exports. Even if we make every effort to ensure that our exported goods are of the highest quality, are subject to all necessary checks and have all the necessary documents, we are in danger of growing protectionism. Corrupt bureaucratic procedures, increased frequency of sanitary checks, requirements for additional certificates, short periods of validity of permits are only some of the examples of discrimination. Our neighbours - Czech Republic, Slovakia and Germany - are at the forefront of creating such barriers to trade. Polish producers estimate that as many as 30% of products that go to these countries are faced with various forms of discrimination. This is a real problem which the Polish government should address as a priority, including in particular the Ministry of Agriculture. Another restriction for Poland is the inefficient functioning of promotion funds, whose primary task should be to promote Polish goods and food on external markets. It is the Polish agricultural producers who finance the activities of the funds, but for a long time I have been told that this activity is not effective. A few months ago I submitted a bill aimed at combining promotion funds into one fund for promotion of Polish agri-food products. It would be obliged to use at least 60% promotion measures outside our country. In my view, the opportunity to maintain and increase Polish exports is the integration of producers and processors and to take joint action to: conquer external markets, new export directions and changes in administrative procedures aimed at simplifying them. I would also like to draw the attention of your Members and the Minister to the fact that these activities should go hand in hand with our activity, our ability to promote our country, to highlight the advantages and to encourage the purchase of Polish agri-food products. The Kukiz Club15 will support this solution. We'll be watching the effects. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u364	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Draft Print Act No 2552 seems to meet the objectives intended for itself. Import requirements, which concern the phytosanitary safety of products of plant origin, are a major challenge for Polish operators. The State Plant Protection and Seed Inspection will be supervised by entities entered in the Register of Exporters. Exporters will have to declare their knowledge of the requirements of third countries, including transit countries, to which they will export their products or through which they will be moved. No administrative decision will be necessary when entering an entry in the register of exporters. This is intended to improve the procedure for entering relevant entities in the register. At the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture, we heard a declaration from the government on advice and support in adapting exporters to the new rules. We hope that the word will be kept. Improving the operation of the State Plant Protection and Seed Inspection will be possible thanks to the extensive cooperation with government authorities and local government and professional self-government units defined in the project. As the National Council of Agricultural Chambers stresses, the proposed provision on this subject, namely Article. The 102th Plant Protection Act gives quite broad interpretation possibilities. However, we see the potential to improve action, to the benefit of food producers, especially when carrying out control or enforcement activities. In the course of the work on the project, the difference between the maximum and the minimum monetary penalty that could have been imposed by the Provincial Plant Protection and Seed Protection Supervisor on the basis of the revised provisions of the Article has been slightly reduced. 108 Plant Protection Act. This range will allow for a precise adjustment of the fine to the seriousness of the specific irregularities. The principles of cooperation between the State Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspection and the State Sanitary Inspection have not been laid down by law, as is the case with the cooperation of the PIORiN and the Trade Quality Inspection of Agricultural and Food Articles and the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. This is justified by the government's failure to report any problems concerning the cooperation of these authorities. We hope that cooperation will be successful, so far, for the benefit of all parties involved in the work process. The Ministry provides that the effects of the proposed changes will not be evaluated until the following year after they have been introduced. Although these changes are intended to open markets in third countries to Polish producers, the scale of exports is intended mainly to depend on trade contacts between Polish operators and their customers. The Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
