The establishment of a medical course at the university mentioned above was already planned many months earlier. In 2014, at Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński University, a very modern laboratory centre was established, providing facilities for basic research in the field of medical sciences. In 2016, the Virtual Anatomy and Surgical Simulation Centre was established. John Paul II. In other fields of education, there are some medical courses that are highly popular among students. In addition, agreements have been signed with different medical centres and a programme has been developed that involves training students at the highest level in the world. The degree of progress of the work and the involvement of the university authorities confirm the need for the establishment of this unit. It is undoubtedly the university's lobbyists who are best acquainted with the needs of students in the field of education. This project also deserves support for typical social reasons. The number of doctors in Poland is steadily increasing year by year, but this increase is disproportionate to the increase in the demand for doctors, including specialists. In addition, according to data from the central register of doctors kept by the General Chamber of Medicine, in Poland for about 172 thousand. doctors and dentists engaged in more than 85 thousand occupations. is over 50 years of age. Given that the retirement age in Poland is 60 years for women and 65 years for men, it should be estimated that about half of the doctors currently employed in 15 years will retire. Meanwhile, in Poland about 4 thousand. Every year, people graduate from medical school. In such a situation, it is justified and necessary to create new medical destinations, and this is what the project envisages. It is also worth noting that the desire of Cardinal Stefan Wyszyński University is that the education of the newly established medical department should be based on high ethical standards. To this end, the university worked closely with the Gemella Clinic in Rome, which is part of the Catholic University of the Sacred Heart in Milan. Creating new directions and medical departments is a good response to the increased demand for various types of medical services in Poland. This also increases the possibility for those who wish to undertake such studies. If all this is combined with sufficiently high ethical standards, it will be very beneficial for both future and current doctors as well as for patients. The project has a positive social impact in terms of increasing the number of doctors and thus the availability of medical services. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is a link between the inhabitants of its village and the authorities of the municipality. The chiefs are passionate people, socialists, people who combine work with activity for the benefit of citizens. The revised Law on Hunting Law, which entered into force on 1 April by the amendment of Mrs Anna Paluch, imposed further obligations on the Chiefs - estimation of hunting damage. Worse still, a very heavy obligation has been imposed on those who do not have experience or adequate competence to assess the damage caused to crops and agricultural foetuses. I would also like to draw attention to the total lack of preparation for the entry into force of the legislation. The law began to apply from 1 April, although the chiefs were not trained and the estimation itself was carried out according to the old guidelines, as no implementing rules have yet been issued. The chiefs also drew attention to imprecise rules. Today's Act proposes that the duties of the Councils be taken over by representatives of the provincial agricultural advisory centers. In my opinion, it's another misguided idea. Several letters have been received by the Committee on Agriculture, from which it is clear that years of extremely modest remuneration for ODR workers have led to the departure of the most valuable workers and numerous staff shortages. Yesterday, during the joint meeting of the Committee on Agriculture and the Environment, I tabled amendments that require representatives of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture to be entrusted with the obligation to estimate hunting damage. I believe that the Agency is an appropriate institution that meets its tasks, because it has a qualified staff team, appropriate competences and trust, which is appropriate to the public institution. I would also add that ARiMR employs more than 11 thousand. employees. It is the largest paying agency in Europe. Over the years, this growing workforce has been needed because of the reluctance of previous governments to properly promote the submission of electronic applications to farmers. Since this year, the situation has changed dramatically, which will release more than 40% in my opinion. staff from the tedious writing of paper-based applications to the electronic system. In addition, the bill does not eliminate numerous shortcomings of the law adopted on 22 March, such as: interference in the self-government of the Polish Association of Hunters and violations of the right of parents to raise children according to their own beliefs, as well as the right of children to privacy. Therefore, I kindly ask Members to reflect deeply on the direction of the changes made and to consider whether the representatives of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture will not perform in a better way the important task of estimating hunting damage. This is possible if the trader has actually identified the deposit and raised the recognition to a category allowing the development of the deposit to be carried out. In turn, the provision of the article. 29 clarifies the grounds for refusing to grant concessions which are too general. Art. 3 in turn, the project contains amendments to the Act of 3 October 2008 on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments. The amendments provide that, in the event of an extension of the concession for the extraction of coal from the deposit within the mining area designated by the existing concession, the requirement to obtain a decision on environmental conditions will not apply. Similar provisions are provided for lignite and, let me remind you, it will be possible for lignite to extend the concession to 6 years. The extension will be one-off for both fuels. The introduction of these changes is justified by the rational economy of fossil deposits, i.e. pursuing the use of all available resources and maintaining the continuity of extraction. Another concession was awarded inefficiently, with delays, with excessive discretion. As a result, there is still no reliable data on the potential of shale gas, which makes it difficult to carry out a realistic raw material policy. The area controlled by NIK is one of the strategic spheres of the economy. The wealth of copper deposits in Poland is estimated at as much as 28 million tons. It is important for almost every branch of the economy and for many areas of everyday life. In turn, exploration and extraction from its own sources of oil or natural gas, including gas from unconventional deposits, in particular shale gas, is of great importance for the prospect of ensuring energy security. The assessment of existing activities in the field of licensing for exploration and recognition of both copper and hydrocarbon deposits, including shale gas, issued by NIK to subsequent ministers was negative. In its report, NIK also points out that, while copper extraction areas have not changed significantly over a controlled period, the area covered by the concessions for shale gas has radically decreased. However, it is the opposite trend - to make further concession areas available to businesses - that would keep the extraction at an appropriate level and increase the scope of search work. This desired trend would be guaranteed by a stable legal situation and a smooth and sound concession procedure on the part of the environmental department. However, the rules have changed several times over a controlled period. In fact, the Resort did not sufficiently secure the civil legal interests of the State Treasury in the agreements for the establishment of mining use for the purpose of exploration and recognition of oil and natural gas deposits and copper deposits. The NIK submitted its comments, which are in fact the answer to the... This law responds to the comments presented by the NIK, which only delights and strengthens your position. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the Modern Club, we are in favour of this Act, but we are paying attention to certain issues. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u468	Dear Marshal, High Seym! I have the honour to present a report to the Rules of Procedure Committee on Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs on the draft law amending the Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator, print No. 2506. The project entered the Sejm on 4 May 2018. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Bureau of the Sejm, sent on 8 May the above draft law to the Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Committee for first reading. The provisions of the Act so far allow for a reduction in the salary of Members and Senators if their conduct prevents the work of the Sejm, the Senate or the National Assembly, as well as their bodies, by gross violation of the relevant regulations, including in the case of unjustified absence and absence of participation in meetings of the Sejm, the Senate, the National Assembly, and their bodies, and in the case of the behaviour of a Member or a Senator who does not respect the seriousness of the Sejm, the Senate and the National Assembly, but only the sitting room. This also applies to the reduction and loss of the right to the parliamentary diet of Members and Senators. The proposed amendments provide for the above mentioned reduction in salary, as well as a parliamentary diet or loss in the event of unworthy behaviour of a Member or a Senator also outside the sitting room, in particular meetings outside the Parliament building, as well as meetings of Parliament committees or parliamentary teams. These regulations aim to increase the prestige and seriousness of the Polish parliament, severely damaged by unworthy behaviour. The first reading of the committee took place on 4 June. The Commission accepted four amendments. Amendments 1 and 3 clarify the issues of behaviour affecting the seriousness of the Sejm, the Senate or the National Assembly not only at their meetings but also at the meetings of their bodies, i.e. Bureau of the Sejm, Senate Bureau, Senior Convention, Sejm and Senate Committees and National Assembly. On the other hand, amendments 2 and 4 extend the provision that infringements in a gross manner of peace or order also apply to the area on the Board of the Senate Office. The Commission, after first reading the draft at its meeting on 4 June 2018 and considering it at its meeting on 7 June 2018, invites the High Sejm to adopt this draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u522	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Why did this bill ever show up? This is a response to what is happening in at least several cities in Poland, and to what the residents are proposing to be concerned about the operation of certain industrial plants. It turns out that some of the products recognized at some point by the Marshals of the voivodships... They're basically so-called. silent consent, without issuing an administrative decision, and in fact merely and exclusively not considering a request made by some traders, allowed certain products and substances not to be considered as waste, possibly even hazardous waste, only to be considered as a by-product which may be subject to further processing, e.g. burning without special precautions. This causes that in the assessment of a large part of the inhabitants e.g. Mielec or Szczecinka are very serious problems with keeping the air clean. Therefore, they have been demanding for a long time to tighten up the provisions of the Waste Act and environmental protection so that what is happening in such plants can be monitored to a much wider extent than before, because so far only sampling has been possible, in fact, during which it is not always possible to determine what is happening in the plant and what kind of substances, perhaps also dangerous, are coming out of the chimneys. On the other hand, these inhabitants demanded a change in the procedure for granting status so-called. by-product for substances or objects arising from different types of production. That is why Members of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, have tabled a bill that responds to such expectations and tries to address the dangers that already exist. The first part provides for the possibility of monitoring to a much wider extent what kind of substances are escaping in plants that process or burn by-products outside the EU Directive, but the EU Directive allows it to do so. In the second part, however, we significantly modify the way in which decisions are made to treat the substances or products concerned as by-products, i.e. those which do not need to have very tight limits on further processing. Instead of the silent consent of the Marshal, as has been the case so far, we introduce the need for a decision by the Marshal of the Voivodeship and in making this decision the Marshal must be assisted by the opinion of the Regional Environmental Inspector, who in special cases can carry out a check to see what the properties of the product are and whether it can be considered as a by-product, or rather should be treated as waste and then more detailed rules should be applied. We also want to ensure that for all by-products that have been reported in Poland so far, and this possibility exists since 2013, the whole procedure has been resumed, because so far practically all notifications - so it is clear from the Ministry of Environment's data - are issued under silent consent, i.e. there was no administrative decision which clearly indicated what type of products and why they can be considered as by-products. We want to make sure that all permits are verified. If they were reported in accordance with the rules and all the data and studies that had to be carried out at this time will not raise doubts, then the next decision of the Marshal of the voivodship in this case should not raise any doubts and should be issued very quickly. According to the Code of Administrative Procedure, there will be 30 days, and in particularly justified cases, 60 days. The presence of a voivodship environmental inspector is intended to prevent products that are hazardous to human health and the environment from being burned in such a way as to prevent the removal of these fatal or dangerous effects for humans. We want this period during which companies interested in obtaining consent to the treatment of a given substance or product as a by-product to have six months to obtain the consent of the Marshal of the voivodship... We understand that in some situations, if the Marshal's decision is negative, businesses should, of course, be able to appeal against such a decision. Hence, we predict that the Minister of the Environment will be the instance of appeal against the decision of the Marshal's office. We want the law to enter into force as soon as possible, so we are proposing here this traditional 14-day deadline. However, as set out in the proposed new legislation, the process of recognising substances as a by-product in which the Regional Environmental Inspector will be involved as an opinion institution and the Marshal of the voivodship as a decision-making body for a maximum of 10 years has been completely distorted. However, in the course of the work on the project, there were many, often valid, reservations in the committees. As noted by the Polish Towns Association, there is a risk that the imposition of additional environmental monitoring parameters on regional municipal waste treatment facilities may result in an increase in prices for waste management, which may be burdened by residents, i.e. residents will have consequences. Art. 1 of the project introduces the possibility of imposing additional measurement obligations in justified cases, but this provision is very vague. It does not specify the specific environmental considerations invoked by the designer. The impreciseness of the rules in practice implies uncertainty in the legal situation of the addressee of the standard. The justification also does not explain the need to impose stricter requirements than those which apply in Europe, i.e. they are sharper than European law expects us to do. The rule leaves the door open to broad interpretation, which certainly does not serve to create and adopt a law that is intended to serve all citizens. The impreciseness of those provisions giving such broad powers to administrative authorities may result in a breach of freedom of economic activity. I hope you're aware of that. In addition, the rules lack time limits for procedures, which can also impact businesses that are legally active. The proposed regulation is not accompanied by an impact assessment to any extent, which was not formally required, because it is a parliamentary project, and this is such a way to circumvent the requirement to include an impact assessment. According to the project so far, the recognition of an object or substance as a by-product will expire 6 months after the date of entry into force. That means a lot of entrepreneurs... Perhaps there will be about 1,500 new decisions, which can cause paralysis of the institution. The projector did not assess whether, in the face of the relatively difficult situation of voivodship environmental inspectors, whose number of employees leave massly, which in effect directly translates into effectiveness and effectiveness, the proposed regulations will not introduce delays, i.e. simply paralysis. Nor has the impact of the regulation on the possible increase in the amount of waste generated been assessed. The change in waste management regulation in Poland is undoubtedly necessary, but the projector completely missed the stage of the impact assessment which he wants to carry out. Such an assessment is urgently needed in this particular case. The absence of an appeal against a decision is contrary to the principle of a two-instant administrative procedure. It's an art. 78 constitutions. There is a refusal to recognise a given object or substance as a by-product if it is not hazardous waste, but suitable for further use. The provisions thus designed may result in an obligation to dispose of waste contrary to the will of the owner, which also undermines the constitutional guarantee of ownership and freedom of business. The projector did not reflect on excessive state interference [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the economy, these are Article. 20 and 22 constitutions. The project does not bring us closer to solving illegal arson problems of garbage dumps, it does not bring us closer to healthier air. But he's gonna implicate a series of legal ambiguities. In this case, we are unable to support this project. Thank you very much. [[It's a massacre! Disgraceful!]]

This problem concerns the whole country, but in a special way Lublin region. According to data from the Lublin Statistical Office in 2016, production of raspberries in the Lublin Voivodeship represented 82.8% the total domestic production. In the district of Kraśnicki, the production of raspberries takes place about 5 thousand. growers. Raspberry plantations take up nearly 4.5,000. ha. The crops are about 36 and 40 thousand. t fruit per year, which accounts for almost half of total domestic production. Kraśnicki County is the largest raspberry hole in the country and one of the two largest in Europe. Farmers growing raspberries, strawberries and other soft fruits to modernise their crops, buy machines, start irrigation systems, often invest in plantations with large amounts of money, and borrow for this purpose. However, at the beginning of this year's season the price of fruit is very low. In the case of raspberries, it is only 2 zlotys per kg, the gooseberry was bought in at 40.50 gr per kg. In the case of raspberries it is three times less than two years ago, and gooseberries - even six times less than last year. In both cases, this is well below the cost of production. This may be the case with many factors. There are votes about price conspiracy and imports of fruit from across the eastern border. In addition, the lack of hands for work is also very often imposed on the difficult price situation. I therefore call on the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development to take decisive action to support soft fruit growers, especially since their condition, including their financial condition, has a very real, very strong impact on the local economy. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-14-sejm-64-1.u566	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Our Seym is really an extraordinary place. Today, the High House has been shaken by a debate completely meaningless. Well, a committee of inquiry has been set up to answer a number of questions that have been well known and known for years. A committee of inquiry has been set up. VAT fraud, which will answer such questions. The questions we know are well-known for what they should sound like. So first of all, the committee is to consider the reasons for VAT fraud. The answer to this question has been well known and known for years. Basically, there are two reasons, as everyone will say, even in a fairly well-informed way, for tax advisors. Firstly, the different VAT rates, secondly, the zero rate for exports, i.e. at the border. In addition, the committee will answer the question of who is guilty of such fraud at all. The answer has been widely known and known for years. The fault is who created the VAT Directives, who created the VAT system, and it is in the EU option. The authors of this directive are responsible for this. If the committee wants to pursue them, let it go to Brussels, conduct a proper investigation and draw consequences on the originators. The committee will then answer the questions why since 2007 the VAT gap has been growing and fraud and VAT carousels have increased. The answer is quite clear and obvious, because in 2004 we entered the European Union and VAT mechanisms, just VAT in the EU option, came into force and, as a result, exactly the same phenomena have come to us, which have been common in the European Union countries for many, many years. There were the same problems, the same ways of avoiding or extorting VAT. We have simply imported this problem, just as we have imported EU solutions. Then the committee will probably try to answer the question of how to deal with these problems, how to deal with VAT fraud, VAT carousels. And here again the answer is obvious and known for years. There are two possible solutions. You can either seal the system very much or completely change it, and you have to immediately notice that very strong sealing of the system will actually cause - because it must cause - that many honest taxpayers will have so many problems and difficulties in clearing VAT and so many obligations that it will actually affect their business. The second solution, which is a fundamental change in the tax system, would actually have to be that we move away from VAT to other solutions, for example. turnover tax, for example in the United States. Then the committee will probably answer the question of how to proceed with extortion. The answer is obvious and known for years, because these mechanisms are widely known in Europe and will probably also be known to us. Simply extortion, VAT carousels will move from one industry to another. In those sectors where they have been blocked, there may be fewer, but in others, in particular. in services, they will certainly develop beautifully.
